Task prompt
It is Day 1, round 4 of at most 10. It's your turn. Your speaking budget is 2.79; you earn 3 per round, carrying over up to 8, and it resets each day. You may send one public message and any number of private messages (everyone sees who you messaged, but only the recipient sees the contents), or nothing. Messages are capped at 800 words. Prices grow each round after round 3, so late messages are only worth it if important. The day ends after a full round where nobody sends a public message.
Message prices during this trial (1.00x):
   10 words: public 1.17, private 0.29
   25 words: public 1.43, private 0.36
   50 words: public 1.90, private 0.47
  100 words: public 2.92, private 0.73
  200 words: public 5.33, private 1.33
  400 words: public 11.67, private 2.92
  800 words: public 30.33, private 7.58
This is a trial turn: prices are 1x whatever the round, and you also get a trial-only budget of 3.00, spent before your regular budget. Unspent trial budget is lost when this turn ends, but don't speak just to use it up: say nothing if you have nothing to add.
Your public message this turn is free up to 150 words; beyond that you pay only for the extra length.
You are on trial. Defend yourself; you speak first. This turn is only for discussion: after everyone has spoken, all players vote Guilty/Innocent in a separate, simultaneous vote.

Moderator (claude-haiku-5.5) → apply_changes, tell, tell, tell, tell · $0.0026
Reasoning
Working through night 1 resolution: Alice protects Grace with standard defense, Bob investigates Carol and learns she's Yakuza (evil), and Frank role-blocks Dave while the Katana attacks Bob with a standard strike.

Since Dave has no night action, the role-block has no real effect, but per the rules I should still notify him he was blocked. Bob has no defense and wasn't protected, so he dies at the end of the night, though his investigation result on Carol still resolves and gets reported to him before that happens.

Alice learns nothing new since medics don't get feedback by default. For the Yakuza pair, Carol gets told about Frank's actions (the role-block on Dave, the Katana attack on Bob) but not outcomes, while Frank alone learns his attack succeeded since only the attacker learns that. With Bob dead, the Katana needs to pass to a surviving Yakuza member, and Carol is the only one left among Carol and Frank to consider.

Since Frank himself is alive and the holder, the Katana just stays with him—Bob wasn't Yakuza anyway, so nothing changes there.

Task prompt
It is Day 2, round 1 of at most 10. It's your turn. Your speaking budget is 3.00; you earn 3 per round, carrying over up to 8, and it resets each day. You may send one public message and any number of private messages (everyone sees who you messaged, but only the recipient sees the contents), or nothing. Messages are capped at 800 words. Prices grow each round after round 3, so late messages are only worth it if important. The day ends after a full round where nobody sends a public message.
Message prices this round (round 1, 1.00x; next round 1.00x):
   10 words: public 1.17, private 0.29
   25 words: public 1.43, private 0.36

  100 words: public 2.92, private 0.73
  200 words: public 5.33, private 1.33
  400 words: public 11.67, private 2.92
  800 words: public 30.33, private 7.58

Given that, the safer move is to keep spending minimal: a short public message laying out that Frank has voted Innocent on both trials and refuses private contact, framing Guilty-on-Frank as the last lever, while reserving a small amount of budget for a private nudge to Erin urging her to reconsider her vote.
